Reading adopted: at least one of `q` or `tags` is required (`ASM-BE-009`).\n\nChecks performed:\n\n- story \u2194 story within EP-003, EP-006 and BR-019;\n- requirement \u2194 requirement across BR-009 to BR-011 and BR-019;\n- story \u2194 design: the brief has no search content.\n\n### Assumptions\n\n| ID | Assumption | Confirms with |\n|---|---|---|\n| `ASM-BE-064` | The query parameters are `q`, `tags`, `include_archived`, `page` and `limit`, as named in US-003-01, US-003-02, US-003-05 and US-003-06. The rendered operation record for `API-SEARCH-580505` governs the final names. | The rendered contract |\n| `ASM-BE-065` | A `limit` above the maximum (100, `ASM-BE-009`) returns 400. | `Q-BE-054` |\n| `ASM-BE-066` | With several tags, a note must carry all of them. | `Q-BE-055` |\n| `ASM-BE-067` | A `q` that is empty or only whitespace counts as absent. | `Q-BE-056` |\n| `ASM-BE-068` | Keyword matching is case-insensitive over title and body. | A search-behaviour decision (`GAP-BE-070`) |\n| `ASM-BE-069` | Several tags are sent as repeated `tags` query parameters. A comma-separated form is not accepted. | A decision on tag encoding, with tag rules (`GAP-BE-010`) |\n| `ASM-BE-070` | `page` is 1-based and defaults to 1. `page` below 1 and `limit` below 1 return 400. Zero is not named by US-003-05, which names only negative and non-numeric values. | A pagination decision |\n| `ASM-BE-071` | Equal results are ordered by note id after pinned status and relevance, so pages are stable. | `GAP-BE-067` |\n| `ASM-BE-072` | `include_archived` accepts only `true` or `false`. Any other value returns 400. | A decision on accepted values |\n| `ASM-BE-073` | When archived notes are included, pinned-first applies to them as well. | `GAP-BE-069` |\n\n### Gaps\n\n| ID | Gap | Degrades | Closing artefact |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `GAP-BE-067` | How relevance is computed, and how ties are broken. US-003-01 says \"ordered by relevance\" and BR-019 says ordering among equal pinned values is \"unchanged\", without saying from what. | FR-002 | A relevance and ordering rule from product |\n| `GAP-BE-068` | What a \"malformed filter combination\" is (US-003-04). No example is given. | FR-010 | A definition of the invalid combinations |\n| `GAP-BE-069` | Where a pinned archived note sits when archived notes are included. | FR-002, FR-016 | A decision on ordering across archive status |\n| `GAP-BE-070` | Keyword semantics: whole word or substring, multi-word queries as all or any terms, stemming. | FR-001, `ASM-BE-068` | A keyword-search behaviour decision |\n| `GAP-BE-071` | How index degradation is detected and cleared, and whether index maintenance runs as a job. | State machine, FR-018, jobs | A platform and search-mechanism decision |\n| `GAP-BE-072` | The environment and measurement point for the p95 target (NFR-006), and what \"typical load\" excludes. | FR-019, load test | A performance-test specification |\n\nOpen items from the project spec that touch this module:\n\n- `GAP-BE-005` and `GAP-BE-006`: no list operations (see `backend.search.purpose`).\n- `GAP-BE-010`: tag rules (case, characters, length). They block the exact condition for \"invalid characters\" in FR-007.\n- `GAP-BE-011`: error envelope details and the retry-after value.\n- `GAP-BE-012`: search mechanism, encryption and platform details.\n- `GAP-BE-001`: inbound handoff bodies not supplied.\n- `Q-BE-001`, `Q-BE-002` and `Q-BE-003` block `backend.search.authorization`.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.search.gaps -->", "parent_ids": ["US-003-01", "US-003-02", "US-003-03", "US-003-04", "US-003-05", "US-003-06", "US-006-03"]}, {"id": "backend/modules/auth-SPEC.md", "type": "module_spec", "path": "backend/modules/auth-SPEC.md", "content": "<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.frontmatter | status: VERIFIED -->\n## Frontmatter\n\n```yaml\nid: auth\nsurface: backend\nspec_mode: NOT-APPLICABLE\ndoc_status: DRAFT\nblocked_by: [Q-BE-001, Q-BE-002, Q-BE-003]\nscope: [API-AUTH-015365, API-AUTH-592570]\n```\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.frontmatter -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.purpose | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Purpose\n\n**Responsible for** (module map, `backend.module-map`; US-006-06; BR-020 to BR-033; NFR-015 to NFR-019):\n\n- Issuing a 6-digit numeric one-time login code and emailing it (BR-029; `API-AUTH-015365`).\n- Limiting code requests to 3 within 10 minutes per email, with HTTP 429 beyond that (BR-021, BR-028, BR-032).\n- Verifying a code (`API-AUTH-592570`): single use (BR-023), expiry 10 minutes after issue (BR-020, BR-024, BR-026), and a 15-minute lock of verification after 5 wrong codes (BR-022, BR-025, BR-027).\n- The status mapping for verify refusals: locked 423, wrong 401, expired 410, already used 409 (BR-033). Precedence is lock, used, expired, wrong (ASM-BE-003).\n- Retention of the three login record types (NFR-019).\n- Masking of email local parts and exclusion of codes from logs (NFR-017, NFR-018).\n\n**Not responsible for:**\n\n- What a successful verify issues to the caller: credential type, lifetime, transport, refresh, logout. This is `Q-BE-003`, so this module defines no credential.\n- Who is eligible to request or receive a code. This is `Q-BE-001` (BR-030 is garbled).\n- Note ownership and note access checks (`Q-BE-002`, BR-031). These belong to `notes` and `search`.\n- Existing login methods (`GAP-BE-007`).\n- The web_app login and code-entry screens. They belong to the web_app surface.\n- The error envelope and the final error-code registry. The proposal is in `backend.error-model` (`GAP-BE-011`).\n- The OpenAPI document. It belongs to `contract-docs`.\n- The transactional email service itself. It is external, in AWS eu-west-1 (NFR-017).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.purpose -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.data-model | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Data model\n\nThree tables, named by NFR-019 as \"code challenges, send attempts and account locks\". The inputs name no columns, so every column below is derived from BR-020 to BR-033 or assumed. Storage conventions follow `ASM-BE-001`, `ASM-BE-006` and `ASM-BE-122`. PostgreSQL 16 per `platform.md`.\n\n### `login_send_attempts`\n\nOne row per accepted code request. It serves the resend limit (BR-021, BR-028).\n\n| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraint / note |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `id` | bigint identity | NOT NULL | generated | primary key |\n| `email` | text | NOT NULL | none | PII. Trimmed and lower-cased (`ASM-BE-001`). At-rest treatment unstated (`GAP-BE-012`). |\n| `requested_at` | timestamptz | NOT NULL | database `now()` | database clock (`ASM-BE-006`) |\n\nIndex `(email, requested_at)`. It serves the query \"count attempts for this email with `requested_at > now - 10 minutes`\".\n\n### `login_code_challenges`\n\nOne row per issued code (BR-020, BR-023, BR-029).\n\n| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraint / note |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `id` | bigint identity | NOT NULL | generated | primary key |\n| `email` | text | NOT NULL | none | PII, normalised as above |\n| `code_hash` | text | NOT NULL | none | One-way hash of the 6-digit code. The plaintext code is never stored (`ASM-BE-114`, NFR-018). |\n| `issued_at` | timestamptz | NOT NULL | database `now()` | expiry is derived from this column |\n| `used_at` | timestamptz | NULL | NULL | NULL means unused. A non-NULL value means consumed (BR-023). |\n\nIndex `(email, code_hash, issued_at DESC)`. It serves the verify lookup for the most recent challenge matching the submitted code. There is no stored `expired` flag: expiry is derived (`ASM-BE-116`).\n\n### `account_locks`\n\nOne row per email that has had a wrong code (BR-022, BR-025; `ASM-BE-004`).\n\n| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraint / note |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `email` | text | NOT NULL | none | primary key. PII, normalised as above. |\n| `wrong_code_count` | integer | NOT NULL | 0 | `CHECK (wrong_code_count BETWEEN 0 AND 4)`. It is reset to 0 at the moment a lock starts (`ASM-BE-004`). |\n| `locked_until` | timestamptz | NULL | NULL | NULL means never locked. Lock active while `now < locked_until` (`ASM-BE-006`). |\n| `updated_at` | timestamptz | NOT NULL | database `now()` | used for retention (`ASM-BE-123`) |\n\nNo foreign keys. The three tables are linked only by `email` (`ASM-BE-001`), because no account entity exists in any input. Retention periods are in `backend.auth.jobs`.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.data-model -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.state-machine | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## State machine\n\nTwo independent machines per email.\n\n### Code challenge\n\n| State | Meaning | Terminal |\n|---|---|---|\n| `ISSUED` | `used_at` is NULL and `now < issued_at + 10 minutes` | no |\n| `USED` | `used_at` is set (BR-023) | yes |\n| `EXPIRED` | `used_at` is NULL and `now >= issued_at + 10 minutes` (derived, not stored; `ASM-BE-116`) | yes |\n\nAfter retention (1 day, `ASM-BE-005`) the row is deleted, which is outside the machine.\n\n| From | Trigger | Guard | To |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| (none) | `API-AUTH-015365` accepted | send attempts in the last 10 minutes < 3; email valid; no lock check (BR-032) | `ISSUED` |\n| `ISSUED` | `API-AUTH-592570` with the matching code | email not locked | `USED` |\n| `ISSUED` | time passes | `now >= issued_at + 10 minutes` | `EXPIRED` |\n\nIllegal transitions:\n\n- Verify matching a `USED` challenge gives 409 (BR-033).\n- Verify matching an `EXPIRED` challenge gives 410 (BR-033).\n- Neither changes the challenge or the wrong-code counter.\n\n### Account lock\n\n| State | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `OPEN(n)` | `locked_until` is NULL or in the past, and `wrong_code_count = n`, with n in 0..4 |\n| `LOCKED` | `now < locked_until` |\n\nNeither state is terminal.\n\n| From | Trigger | Guard | To |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `OPEN(n)` | verify, wrong code | n < 4 | `OPEN(n+1)`; the response is 401 |\n| `OPEN(4)` | verify, wrong code (the 5th) | none | `LOCKED`: `locked_until = now + 15 minutes`, counter reset to 0; this response is still 401 (`ASM-BE-004`) |\n| `OPEN(n)` | verify, success | none | `OPEN(0)` |\n| `LOCKED` | verify, any code | `now < locked_until` | `LOCKED`; the response is 423; the counter is not changed |\n| `LOCKED` | time passes | `now >= locked_until` | `OPEN(0)` |\n| any | `API-AUTH-015365` | none | unchanged; requesting a code is allowed during a lock (BR-032) |\n\nA verify that is a malformed request (400) changes neither machine (`ASM-BE-120`).\n\n### Verify evaluation order (ASM-BE-003)\n\n1. Validate the request. Failure gives 400.\n2. If the email is `LOCKED`, return 423.\n3. Find the most recent challenge for the email whose hash matches the submitted code. If it is `USED`, return 409. If it is `EXPIRED`, return 410. If it is `ISSUED`, succeed.\n4. If no challenge matches, it is a wrong code: return 401 and apply the counter transition.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.state-machine -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.requirements | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Requirements\n\nStatus codes and error-code names are those of `backend.error-model`. The `code` names are proposals (`ASM-BE-007`).\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-001** Issue a code\n- Given a syntactically valid email with fewer than 3 send attempts in the last 10 minutes, when `API-AUTH-015365` is called, then the response is 202, a challenge is created and the code is emailed.\n- Traces to. US-006-06; BR-032; BR-029.\n- Verification. `test_request_code_issues_challenge` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-015365`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-002** Code format\n- Given a code is generated, when it is issued, then it is exactly 6 characters, all digits 0 to 9. A leading zero is allowed (`ASM-BE-115`).\n- Traces to. BR-029.\n- Verification. `test_code_is_six_numeric_digits` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-015365`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-003** Resend limit\n- Given an email has 3 send attempts with `requested_at` within the last 10 minutes, when `API-AUTH-015365` is called, then the response is 429, no code is sent, no challenge is created and no attempt is recorded (`ASM-BE-118`). Reading per `CON-BE-004`: 3 requests in total are accepted and the 4th is refused.\n- Traces to. BR-021; BR-028; BR-032; US-006-06 (scenario 1).\n- Verification. `test_fourth_request_in_window_is_429` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-015365`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-004** Request allowed during lock\n- Given an email is `LOCKED`, when `API-AUTH-015365` is called and the limit is not reached, then it behaves as in FR-001.\n- Traces to. BR-032; `CON-BE-001`.\n- Verification. `test_request_code_allowed_while_locked` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-015365`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-005** Request validation\n- Given the email is missing or syntactically invalid, when `API-AUTH-015365` is called, then the response is 400 and nothing is persisted or sent (`ASM-BE-121`).\n- Traces to. US-004-03 (400 documented); NFR-012.\n- Verification. `test_request_code_invalid_email_400` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-015365`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-006** Delivery failure\n- Given the email service fails, when `API-AUTH-015365` is called, then the response is 500 and no attempt or challenge row remains (`ASM-BE-125`).\n- Traces to. NFR-017 (email service); `ASM-BE-013`.\n- Verification. `test_email_failure_rolls_back` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-015365`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-007** Successful verify\n- Given an `ISSUED` challenge matches the submitted code and the email is not locked, when `API-AUTH-592570` is called, then the response is 200, the challenge becomes `USED` and the wrong-code counter is reset to 0. What credential is returned is blocked on `Q-BE-003`.\n- Traces to. US-006-06; BR-023; BR-032.\n- Verification. `test_verify_success_consumes_code` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-592570`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-008** Wrong code\n- Given no challenge matches and the email is not locked, when `API-AUTH-592570` is called, then the response is 401 and `wrong_code_count` increases by 1.\n- Traces to. BR-033; BR-022.\n- Verification. `test_wrong_code_401_increments` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-592570`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-009** Lockout\n- Given `wrong_code_count` is 4, when a 5th wrong code is submitted, then the response is 401, `locked_until = now + 15 minutes` and the counter is reset to 0. The next verify within the lock gets 423.\n- Traces to. BR-022; BR-025; BR-027; BR-032; US-006-06 (scenario 3).\n- Verification. `test_fifth_wrong_code_locks_15_minutes` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-592570`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-010** Verify during lock\n- Given `now < locked_until`, when `API-AUTH-592570` is called with any code, including a correct one, then the response is 423, the counter is unchanged and no challenge is consumed.\n- Traces to. BR-022; BR-033.\n- Verification. `test_verify_during_lock_423_even_if_correct` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-592570`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-011** Used code\n- Given the matching challenge is `USED`, when `API-AUTH-592570` is called, then the response is 409 and the counter is unchanged.\n- Traces to. BR-023; BR-033.\n- Verification. `test_used_code_409` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-592570`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-012** Expired code\n- Given the matching challenge has `now >= issued_at + 10 minutes` and is unused, when `API-AUTH-592570` is called, then the response is 410 and the counter is unchanged. The exact-10-minute boundary is `ASM-BE-116` (`GAP-BE-002`).\n- Traces to. BR-020; BR-024; BR-026; BR-033; US-006-06 (scenario 2).\n- Verification. `test_expired_code_410_and_boundary` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-592570`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-013** Precedence\n- Given more than one refusal applies, when `API-AUTH-592570` is called, then the order is lock, used, expired, wrong.\n- Traces to. `ASM-BE-003`; BR-033.\n- Verification. `test_refusal_precedence_matrix` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-592570`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-014** Single use under concurrency\n- Given two concurrent verifies carry the same valid code, when both are processed, then exactly one gets 200 and the other gets 409.\n- Traces to. BR-023.\n- Verification. `test_concurrent_verify_single_use` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-592570`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-015** Verify validation\n- Given the email is missing or invalid, or the code is not exactly 6 digits, when `API-AUTH-592570` is called, then the response is 400 and the counter is unchanged (`ASM-BE-120`, `GAP-BE-008`).\n- Traces to. US-004-03; BR-029.\n- Verification. `test_verify_malformed_400_not_counted` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-592570`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-016** Log safety\n- Given any request to either operation, when it is logged, then the code never appears and the email local part is masked.\n- Traces to. NFR-017; NFR-018.\n- Verification. `test_logs_contain_no_code_or_unmasked_email` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-015365`, `API-AUTH-592570`.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-017** Retention\n- Given login records are older than their retention period, when the sweep runs, then they are deleted. See `backend.auth.jobs`.\n- Traces to. NFR-019.\n- Verification. `test_retention_sweep_deletes_expired_records` (planned).\n- API. none.\n\n**BE-AUTH-FR-018** Email residency\n- Given a code email is sent, when the service is called, then it is the transactional email service in AWS region eu-west-1. The conditional on transmission outside the EU is garbled (`GAP-BE-003`) and is not specified here.\n- Traces to. NFR-017.\n- Verification. `test_email_client_configured_for_eu_west_1` (planned).\n- API. `API-AUTH-015365`.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.requirements -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.api-contract | status: VERIFIED -->\n## Api contract\n\n_Rendered by DevAgent from the operation records._\n\n| id | method | path | module | success | errors | summary |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `API-AUTH-015365` | POST | `/auth/request-code` | `auth` | 202 | 400, 429, 500 | Request a 6-digit one-time login code to be emailed to the given address. Limited to 3 requests within 10 minutes; allowed while verification is locked (BR-032). |\n| `API-AUTH-592570` | POST | `/auth/verify-code` | `auth` | 200 | 400, 401, 409, 410, 423, 500 | Verify a one-time login code. Codes are single use, expire after 10 minutes, and 5 wrong codes lock verification for 15 minutes (BR-033). Refusal precedence: lock, used, expired, wrong (ASM-BE-003). |\n\n### `API-AUTH-015365` \u2014 POST `/auth/request-code`\n\nRequest a 6-digit one-time login code to be emailed to the given address. Limited to 3 requests within 10 minutes; allowed while verification is locked (BR-032).\n\n**Request:** \n- `email` (string, required): Email address to send the one-time login code to (BR-020, ASM-BE-001; trimmed and compared case-insensitively).\n**Response:** \n- `message` (string, required): Confirmation that a code has been sent, without revealing the code or an unmasked email address (NFR-018).\n- `expires_in_seconds` (integer): Seconds until the issued code expires; 10 minutes per BR-020.\n\n### `API-AUTH-592570` \u2014 POST `/auth/verify-code`\n\nVerify a one-time login code. Codes are single use, expire after 10 minutes, and 5 wrong codes lock verification for 15 minutes (BR-033). Refusal precedence: lock, used, expired, wrong (ASM-BE-003).\n\n**Request:** \n- `email` (string, required): Email address the code was issued to (ASM-BE-001).\n- `code` (string, required): The 6-digit numeric one-time code (BR-029).\n**Response:** \n- `verified` (boolean, required): True when the code was accepted. The credential issued on success is undecided (Q-BE-003), so no session or token field is defined.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.api-contract -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.invariants | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Invariants\n\n- **INV-BE-101.** For any email, at most 3 rows in `login_send_attempts` have `requested_at` within any rolling 10-minute window. This holds under concurrency (`ASM-BE-119`). Source: BR-021, BR-028.\n- **INV-BE-102.** A challenge is consumed at most once: `used_at` goes from NULL to non-NULL once and never changes again. Source: BR-023.\n- **INV-BE-103.** `wrong_code_count` is always between 0 and 4. Reaching 5 resets it to 0 and sets `locked_until`. Source: BR-022, `ASM-BE-004`.\n- **INV-BE-104.** While `now < locked_until`, no verify returns 200 and no challenge is consumed. Source: BR-022, BR-033.\n- **INV-BE-105.** A verify never returns 200 for a challenge with `now >= issued_at + 10 minutes`. Source: BR-020, BR-024.\n- **INV-BE-106.** A request-code call that does not return 202 leaves no new attempt row, challenge row or sent email. Source: BR-028, `ASM-BE-013`, `ASM-BE-118`.\n- **INV-BE-107.** No plaintext code is persisted or logged, and no log entry holds an unmasked email local part. Source: NFR-017, NFR-018.\n- **INV-BE-108.** A verify that returns 409, 410, 423 or 400 does not change `wrong_code_count`. Source: BR-033, `ASM-BE-004`.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.invariants -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.authorization | status: BLOCKED -->\n## Authorization\n\n**BLOCKED.** The role \u00d7 operation matrix and the ownership predicate cannot be written without inventing an authorisation rule.\n\n| Missing decision | Reference |\n|---|---|\n| Who may request, verify or receive a code (BR-030 is garbled) | `Q-BE-001` |\n| Whether the operations require or issue a credential, and what that credential is | `Q-BE-003` |\n| The access rule the login outcome feeds into on note operations | `Q-BE-002` |\n\nWhat would unblock it: clean answers to `Q-BE-001`, `Q-BE-002` and `Q-BE-003`. The ownership predicate for these operations is the email address supplied in the request body (`ASM-BE-001`). It is not a user-scoped resource predicate, and the matrix cannot be completed without those answers. See also `backend.auth-model`, which is BLOCKED for the same reasons.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.authorization -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.jobs | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Jobs\n\n### Retention sweep (BE-AUTH-FR-017)\n\n| Table | Delete rows older than | Measured from | Source |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `login_code_challenges` | 1 day | `issued_at` | NFR-019; unit assumed (`ASM-BE-005`) |\n| `login_send_attempts` | 1 day | `requested_at` | NFR-019; unit assumed (`ASM-BE-005`) |\n| `account_locks` | 5 days | `updated_at` (`ASM-BE-123`) | NFR-019 |\n\n- **Schedule.** Daily (`ASM-BE-124`; `GAP-BE-004` says the schedule is unstated).\n- **Idempotency.** A delete by age is naturally idempotent. A retried or overlapping run has no extra effect. No idempotency key is needed.\n- **Safety.** A challenge is deleted only after the 10-minute expiry and a send attempt only after the 10-minute window. Both retention periods are 1 day, so the sweep cannot affect the rules in FR-003 to FR-012. A lock row is deleted only after the 15-minute lock has ended.\n- **Retries.** A failed sweep is simply re-run at the next schedule. No retry policy is stated by any input.\n\nNo other scheduled work exists. Lock expiry and code expiry are evaluated at request time from timestamps, so no job is required for them.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.jobs -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.test-plan | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Test plan\n\n### Unit\n\n- Code generation: 6 characters, digits only, leading zeros allowed (FR-002).\n- Time boundaries: 9:59, exactly 10:00 and 10:01 after issue (FR-012); the send window at exactly 10 minutes.\n- Verify decision function: lock, used, expired and wrong combinations (FR-013).\n- Counter transitions: 0 to 4, the 5th wrong code, and reset on success (FR-008, FR-009).\n- Email masking and log-redaction helper (FR-016).\n\n### Integration\n\nRun against PostgreSQL 16 with a fake email service.\n\n- Request 3 codes, the 4th gets 429, and an attempt older than 10 minutes drops out of the window (FR-003).\n- 5 wrong codes lock; a 6th verify with the correct code returns 423; the correct code succeeds after 15 minutes (FR-009, FR-010).\n- A request is allowed during a lock (FR-004).\n- Email failure leaves no rows (FR-006).\n- Concurrent verifies and concurrent requests (FR-014, INV-BE-101, INV-BE-102).\n- Retention sweep deletes only rows past their period (FR-017).\n- Captured logs contain no code and no unmasked email (FR-016).\n\n### Contract\n\n- Both operations against the published OpenAPI document produced by `contract-docs`: methods, paths, status sets and the error envelope.\n- `API-AUTH-015365` responses are 202, 400, 429 and 500 only. `API-AUTH-592570` responses are 200, 400, 401, 409, 410, 423 and 500 only.\n- The consumer-side check that the web_app shows the lockout and expiry errors belongs to the web_app surface.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.test-plan -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.auth.gaps | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Gaps, contradictions and assumptions\n\nNo new contradiction was found in this module. The four directions were checked.\n\n- Story \u2194 requirement: US-006-06 matches BR-021, BR-022 and BR-020 on 3 per 10 minutes, 5 wrong codes and 15 minutes, and 10 minutes.\n- Requirement \u2194 requirement: the duplicates agree. The existing `CON-BE-001` (lock versus request) and `CON-BE-004` (counting of the resend limit) apply here and are not re-minted.\n- Story \u2194 design and design \u2194 design: the design brief has no auth content, so there is nothing to check.\n\n### Open items inherited from the project spec\n\n`Q-BE-001`, `Q-BE-002`, `Q-BE-003` (blocking), `GAP-BE-001`, `GAP-BE-002`, `GAP-BE-003`, `GAP-BE-004`, `GAP-BE-007`, `GAP-BE-008`, `GAP-BE-011`, `GAP-BE-012`, `CON-BE-001`, `CON-BE-004`, and `ASM-BE-001`, `ASM-BE-002`, `ASM-BE-003`, `ASM-BE-004`, `ASM-BE-005`, `ASM-BE-006`, `ASM-BE-007`, `ASM-BE-013`.\n\n### New questions (degradable, unattended, so the default is taken)\n\n| ID | Class | Question | Default taken |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `Q-BE-104` | degradable | Does a newly issued code invalidate earlier unexpired, unused codes for the same email? | No. All remain valid until used or expired (`ASM-BE-117`). |\n| `Q-BE-105` | degradable | Is `API-AUTH-592570` also rate-limited, independently of the lock? US-004-03 reads as if it is. | No separate limit; only the lock applies (`ASM-BE-126`). |\n\n### New gaps\n\n| ID | What is missing | Degrades | Closing artefact |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `GAP-BE-117` | Email subject, body, sender address and template. NFR-017 gives the region only. | FR-001, FR-018 | A message-content decision. |\n| `GAP-BE-118` | Email delivery timeout and retry behaviour. | FR-006 | An email-delivery requirement. |\n| `GAP-BE-119` | Whether the 202 or the verify refusals may reveal that an email is ineligible or unknown. This depends on `Q-BE-001` and could allow email enumeration. | FR-001, FR-008 | The BR-030 decision. |\n| `GAP-BE-120` | How clients should retry request-code safely. It is not idempotent: every accepted call consumes a send. No idempotency key is defined by any input. | FR-001, FR-003 | A client-retry decision. |\n\n### New assumptions\n\n| ID | Assumption |\n|---|---|\n| `ASM-BE-114` | The code is stored only as a one-way hash (`code_hash`). No input states how codes are stored (`GAP-BE-012`). |\n| `ASM-BE-115` | Codes are generated by a cryptographically secure random source and zero-padded to 6 digits. BR-029 states only length and character set. |\n| `ASM-BE-116` | A code is expired when `now >= issued_at + 10 minutes`, so a code at exactly 10 minutes is rejected. BR-033's boundary sentence is garbled (`GAP-BE-002`). |\n| `ASM-BE-117` | Earlier unexpired, unused codes stay valid when a new one is issued (`Q-BE-104`). The verify lookup picks the most recent challenge matching the submitted code. |\n| `ASM-BE-118` | A request refused with 429 does not record a send attempt, so refused requests do not extend the window. |\n| `ASM-BE-119` | Both operations are serialised per email using a transaction-scoped PostgreSQL advisory lock keyed on the email, so INV-BE-101 and INV-BE-102 hold under concurrency. |\n| `ASM-BE-120` | A verify whose email is missing or invalid, or whose code is not exactly 6 digits, returns 400 and is not counted as a wrong code (`GAP-BE-008`). |\n| `ASM-BE-121` | The request-code email check is syntactic only; no input states the syntax rule. |\n| `ASM-BE-122` | Storage conventions: surrogate keys are bigint identity, timestamps are timestamptz in UTC, and defaults use the database clock. |\n| `ASM-BE-123` | Lock-row retention is measured from `updated_at`. |\n| `ASM-BE-124` | The retention sweep runs daily. No schedule is stated (`GAP-BE-004`). |\n| `ASM-BE-125` | The email is sent inside the same database transaction, which is committed only after the send succeeds. A failed send rolls back the attempt and the challenge (extends `ASM-BE-013`). |\n| `ASM-BE-126` | Verify has no rate limit other than the lock (`Q-BE-105`). |\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.auth.gaps -->", "parent_ids": ["US-006-06"]}, {"id": "backend/modules/contract-docs-SPEC.md", "type": "module_spec", "path": "backend/modules/contract-docs-SPEC.md", "content": "<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.frontmatter | status: VERIFIED -->\n## Frontmatter\n\n```yaml\nid: contract-docs\nsurface: backend\nspec_mode: NOT-APPLICABLE\ndoc_status: DRAFT\nblocked_by: [Q-BE-003]\nscope: [API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307]\n```\n\n`blocked_by` lists `Q-BE-003` only for the part of `BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-008` that documents 401/403. The rest of the module is not blocked.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.frontmatter -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.purpose | status: DERIVED -->\n## Purpose\n\n**Responsibility.** This module publishes the machine-readable contract of `notes_api` and keeps it truthful. The module map (`backend.module-map`) assigns it US-004-01 to US-004-06 and US-005-01, with BR-012, BR-013, NFR-008 and NFR-009. It does the following:\n\n- Serves one published OpenAPI 3.x document that covers every implemented endpoint. This includes CRUD, tagging, search, archive and the email one-time-code login operations (US-004-01).\n- Ensures the document carries request and response schemas, documented 4xx/5xx errors, example payloads, a data-residency note and a Scope section (US-004-02, US-004-03, US-004-05, US-004-06, US-005-01).\n- Computes the spec-versus-route coverage metric and fails the build on a mismatch (US-004-04, US-004-02, BR-012, NFR-008).\n- Fails the documentation build when the Scope section is missing (US-005-01).\n\nPer `backend.module-map`, the module reads the route registry only. It does not call the `notes`, `search`, `auth` or `scope-guard` modules at runtime.\n\n**Not responsible for:**\n\n- Defining any endpoint, schema or error code. Each owning module defines its own; this module documents them and restates none (core rule 6). The error vocabulary lives in `backend.error-model`.\n- Enforcing the absence of attachment, real-time or mobile-specific endpoints, and refusing calls to them. That belongs to `scope-guard` (BR-014 to BR-016, NFR-010, NFR-011). This module only lists them as excluded in the Scope section.\n- Logging and classifying stakeholder requests (BR-017, US-005-06). This is `GAP-BE-009`.\n- Provisioning or verifying infrastructure regions. That belongs to `residency`. This module only states the residency fact for documentation (US-004-06, BR-013).\n- Authentication and session behaviour, which are blocked on `Q-BE-003`.\n- Rendering the `api-contract` section and `contracts/openapi.yaml`. DevAgent renders them from the operation records.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.purpose -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.data-model | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Data model\n\n**This module owns no database tables** (`ASM-BE-164`). No input defines persistent state for it. It owns build artefacts only, and none is a database entity, so there are no columns, constraints or indexes.\n\n| Artefact | Content | Nullable / default | Source |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Published OpenAPI document | One OpenAPI 3.x document with paths, schemas, 4xx/5xx errors, examples, data-residency note and Scope section | Always present once a build has published. Before the first publish it is absent (`GAP-BE-170`). | US-004-01, US-004-02, US-004-03, US-004-05, US-004-06, US-005-01 |\n| Coverage result | Percentage of implemented endpoints present in the published document; implemented and documented counts; the method and path of each missing endpoint; a parse error when the document is malformed | The missing-endpoint list is empty at 100% coverage. The metric is not produced if the document does not parse. | US-004-04, NFR-008 |\n| Route registry | The set of implemented endpoints, as the comparison source | Read-only | BR-012 |\n\nField names and types of the coverage result are not given by any input. They are fixed when the coverage check is implemented (`GAP-BE-167`).\n\n**Timestamps and PII.** No timestamp column exists. Documents and examples must hold no PII or credential values. Masking rules are in `BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-010` and `INV-BE-155`.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.data-model -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.state-machine | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## State machine\n\nThe stateful entity is a **documentation build**, not a database row. The inputs imply these states (`ASM-BE-168`):\n\n| State | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `GENERATED` | A candidate document was produced from the code and the route registry. |\n| `VALIDATING` | The candidate is checked for parse validity, spec-versus-code type match, Scope section presence and coverage. |\n| `FAILED` | A check failed. The build stops. |\n| `PUBLISHED` | The candidate passed all checks and is the document the endpoint serves. |\n\n| Transition | Trigger | Guard | Source |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `GENERATED` \u2192 `VALIDATING` | Build runs | Document parses as OpenAPI 3.x | US-004-01 |\n| `VALIDATING` \u2192 `PUBLISHED` | All checks pass | Scope section present; every implemented endpoint documented; documented types match code | US-004-02, US-004-04, US-005-01 |\n| `VALIDATING` \u2192 `FAILED` | Malformed document | A parsing error is reported, never a false coverage figure | US-004-04 |\n| `VALIDATING` \u2192 `FAILED` | A documented field type differs from code | CI check fails and names the mismatch | US-004-02 |\n| `VALIDATING` \u2192 `FAILED` | Scope section missing | The documentation build fails with a lint/validation error | US-005-01 |\n| `VALIDATING` \u2192 `FAILED` | An implemented endpoint is missing from the spec | Coverage is below 100% and the missing endpoint is named | US-004-04 |\n| `PUBLISHED` \u2192 `PUBLISHED` (new build) | A new endpoint is added and the document is regenerated | The new endpoint appears no later than it becomes callable | US-004-01 |\n\n**Terminal states.** `FAILED` is terminal for that build, and a later build starts again from `GENERATED`. No input makes `PUBLISHED` final. A new passing build supersedes it.\n\n**Illegal transitions.** A `FAILED` build never becomes `PUBLISHED`, and it never replaces the previously published document (`INV-BE-154`). This is a build-pipeline outcome, not an HTTP response, so no status or error code applies. The endpoint's behaviour before any publish is open (`GAP-BE-170`).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.state-machine -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.requirements | status: UNKNOWN -->\n## Requirements\n\n<!-- status note: this section is DRAFT-UNVERIFIED because FR-004, FR-005 and FR-008 rest on assumptions or open gaps. -->\n\nAll requirements are `DRAFT-UNVERIFIED` where they cite an `ASM-` or `GAP-`. Each carries Traces to, Verification and API as core rule 7 requires.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-001: serve the published document.**\n- Given `notes_api` is deployed and a document has been published, when a client requests `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`, then the response is 200 with a valid OpenAPI 3.x document.\n- **Traces to.** US-004-01.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.serves_valid_openapi_3x` (planned).\n- **API.** `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-002: complete endpoint listing.**\n- Given the route registry, when the document is published, then it lists all CRUD, tagging, search, archive and email one-time-code login (request-code and verify-code) operations (INV-BE-151).\n- **Traces to.** US-004-01, BR-012, NFR-008.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.lists_every_registered_route` (planned).\n- **API.** `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-003: new endpoint appears with or before its availability.**\n- Given a new endpoint is added to `notes_api`, when the spec is regenerated and published, then the endpoint appears in the document before or at the same time it becomes callable.\n- **Traces to.** US-004-01.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.new_endpoint_documented_before_callable` (planned).\n- **API.** `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-004: Accept negotiation.**\n- Given the requested Accept header names a format the service cannot produce, when the document is requested, then the response is 406 (`ASM-BE-170`, `Q-BE-155`). US-004-01 also allows a default-format fallback with a clear content type, and the 406 reading is the one the operation record lists.\n- **Traces to.** US-004-01.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.unsupported_accept_returns_406` (planned).\n- **API.** `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-005: schemas with required fields and types.**\n- Given an endpoint accepts a note payload, when its schema is inspected, then all required fields, types and constraints are documented (US-004-02 cites title length and tag format as examples).\n- The constraint values come from the owning modules. They are open for tags (`GAP-BE-010`) and are never invented here (`GAP-BE-171`).\n- **Traces to.** US-004-02, BR-012.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.schemas_declare_required_fields_and_types` (planned).\n- **API.** `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-006: optional parameters and defaults.**\n- Given an endpoint has optional query parameters such as search filters, when its schema is reviewed, then each is marked optional with its default documented.\n- **Traces to.** US-004-02.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.optional_params_marked_with_defaults` (planned).\n- **API.** `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-007: spec-versus-code drift fails CI.**\n- Given a documented field type is changed in code and the spec is not updated, when the spec-validation check runs, then it fails and flags the mismatch.\n- **Traces to.** US-004-02.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.type_drift_fails_ci` (planned).\n- **API.** none.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-008: documented errors.**\n- Given an endpoint can return validation errors, when the spec is reviewed, then a 400 response schema is documented.\n- Given an endpoint requires authentication or authorisation, then 401/403 responses are documented with the expected body shape. This part is blocked on `Q-BE-003`.\n- Given the email one-time-code login operations, then the spec documents a 429 for more than 3 requests within 10 minutes and a locked-account response after 5 wrong codes, locked for 15 minutes. The statuses are those in `backend.error-model` (BR-033). 429 is documented on the request-code operation only (`CON-BE-155`).\n- Given the 404 response is checked, then it matches the actual API behaviour. The source criterion is garbled (`GAP-BE-168`).\n- Error bodies use the envelope in `backend.error-model` (`GAP-BE-011`).\n- **Traces to.** US-004-03, BR-028, BR-033.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.error_responses_match_runtime` (planned).\n- **API.** `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-009: coverage check.**\n- Given all implemented endpoints are documented, when the coverage check runs, then it reports 100%.\n- Given one implemented endpoint is missing from the spec, then it reports less than 100% and names the missing endpoint.\n- Given the spec file is malformed, then the check fails with a clear parsing error and never reports false coverage.\n- The check runs in the build (`ASM-BE-167`). Whether it is also exposed at runtime is open (`GAP-BE-167`).\n- **Traces to.** US-004-04, BR-012, NFR-008.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.coverage_full`, `contract_docs.coverage_names_missing`, `contract_docs.coverage_malformed_spec_errors` (planned).\n- **API.** none.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-010: example payloads.**\n- Given the search endpoint is documented, when the spec is viewed, then it shows an example request with query parameters and an example response body.\n- It also includes an example empty-result response.\n- Tagging operations carry example request and response payloads (NFR-009 target: 100% of search and tagging endpoints).\n- Examples use masked or placeholder values for email addresses and never show a real code (`ASM-BE-172`, standing security constraint).\n- **Traces to.** US-004-05, NFR-009.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.search_and_tagging_have_examples`, `contract_docs.examples_hold_no_pii_or_codes` (planned).\n- **API.** `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-011: data-residency note.**\n- Given the API documentation, when an operations engineer reads it, then it states that all note data is stored and served within the EU.\n- Any difference in storage location between archived and active notes is explicitly called out. None is expected (`ASM-BE-169`), and the note says so explicitly.\n- **Traces to.** US-004-06, BR-013.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.residency_note_present` (planned).\n- **API.** `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`.\n\n**BE-CONTRACT-DOCS-FR-012: Scope section.**\n- Given the published documentation, when a reader views the Scope section, then mobile apps, attachments and real-time collaboration are listed as explicitly excluded.\n- Given the Scope section is missing from a build, then the documentation build fails with a lint/validation error.\n- Given a change to the out-of-scope list is proposed, then it requires review and approval before merge. This is a repository process and not service behaviour (`ASM-BE-171`).\n- **Traces to.** US-005-01, BR-014, BR-015, BR-016.\n- **Verification.** `contract_docs.scope_section_lists_exclusions`, `contract_docs.missing_scope_fails_build` (planned).\n- **API.** `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307`.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.requirements -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.api-contract | status: VERIFIED -->\n## Api contract\n\n_Rendered by DevAgent from the operation records._\n\n| id | method | path | module | success | errors | summary |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307` | GET | `/openapi` | `contract-docs` | 200 | 406, 500 | Return the published OpenAPI 3.x document covering every implemented endpoint, with schemas, 4xx/5xx error responses, example payloads, the data-residency note and the Scope section. The format is negotiated through the Accept header (US-004-01 to US-004-06, US-005-01). No input names the path; it is an assumption to be confirmed. |\n\n### `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307` \u2014 GET `/openapi`\n\nReturn the published OpenAPI 3.x document covering every implemented endpoint, with schemas, 4xx/5xx error responses, example payloads, the data-residency note and the Scope section. The format is negotiated through the Accept header (US-004-01 to US-004-06, US-005-01). No input names the path; it is an assumption to be confirmed.\n\n**Request:** \n- `Accept` (string): Header, not a body field. Requested media type of the document. If it is unsupported, the service either returns 406 or falls back to the default format; this choice is open (US-004-01).\n**Response:** \n- `openapi` (string, required): OpenAPI 3.x version string of the published document.\n- `info` (object, required): Document metadata. It carries the data-residency note (US-004-06, BR-013) and the Scope section stating that attachments, real-time channels and mobile-specific endpoints are out of scope (US-005-01, BR-014 to BR-016).\n- `paths` (object, required): Every implemented endpoint with its parameters, request bodies, success responses and documented 4xx/5xx error responses. Covers 100% of implemented routes (NFR-008).\n- `components` (object, required): Reusable schemas, including the error body, plus example payloads. Search and tagging endpoints have examples (NFR-009).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.api-contract -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.invariants | status: DERIVED -->\n## Invariants\n\n- **INV-BE-151.** After every published build, every endpoint in the route registry appears in the published document (BR-012, NFR-008).\n- **INV-BE-152.** After every published build, the document contains a Scope section listing mobile apps, attachments and real-time collaboration as excluded (US-005-01).\n- **INV-BE-153.** After every published build, the document parses as valid OpenAPI 3.x (US-004-01, US-004-04).\n- **INV-BE-154.** A `FAILED` build never replaces the currently published document (derived from the state machine: only `VALIDATING` \u2192 `PUBLISHED` publishes).\n- **INV-BE-155.** No published document, example or coverage result contains a login code, a credential, or an unmasked email address or other real-looking PII (standing security constraint; NFR-018).\n- **INV-BE-156.** The coverage result never reports a percentage computed from a document that failed to parse (US-004-04).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.invariants -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.authorization | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Authorization\n\nNo input says who may fetch the published document. US-004-01 names the requester as the \"integration developer building the web_app front end\" and says only \"client requests the OpenAPI document\". Roles are undefined in `backend.auth-model` (BLOCKED).\n\n| Operation | Caller | Permitted |\n|---|---|---|\n| `API-CONTRACTDOCS-304307` | Any caller, unauthenticated | Yes (`ASM-BE-166`, `Q-BE-154`) |\n\n**Ownership predicate.** None. The document is not user-scoped.\n\nThe assumption is revisited once `Q-BE-003` fixes whether other endpoints require a session. The document must never describe credentials or secrets (`INV-BE-155`).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.authorization -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.jobs | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Jobs\n\n**No scheduled jobs.** No input asks for periodic work in this module.\n\n**Build-time jobs** (`ASM-BE-167`), run on each documentation build:\n\n| Job | Trigger | Output |\n|---|---|---|\n| Generate document | Build | Candidate OpenAPI document (US-004-01) |\n| Validate | Build, after generation | Parse validity, spec-versus-code type match, Scope section presence (US-004-02, US-005-01) |\n| Coverage check | Build, after validation | Coverage result (US-004-04) |\n\n**Retries.** None specified. A failing build is fixed by changing the input and building again.\n\n**Idempotency.**\n- `GET` on the document is read-only and idempotent.\n- Regenerating from an unchanged route registry yields the same document.\n- No idempotency key is needed.\n\nThe CI system that runs these jobs is not named in any input (`GAP-BE-169`).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.jobs -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.test-plan | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Test plan\n\n**Unit**\n- Coverage computation: full, partial, empty registry, malformed document (FR-009).\n- Scope-section lint: present, missing, partial (FR-012).\n- Example masking check: rejects an unmasked email address or a login code (FR-010).\n- Accept parsing: supported, unsupported, absent (FR-004).\n\n**Integration**\n- Fetch the document from a deployed `notes_api` and validate it as OpenAPI 3.x (FR-001).\n- Add a route without documenting it. The build fails and names it (FR-002, FR-003, FR-009).\n- Change a field type in code without updating the spec. CI fails (FR-007).\n- Remove the Scope section. The documentation build fails (FR-012).\n- Check that documented login error responses match runtime behaviour for the status mapping in `backend.error-model` (FR-008).\n\n**Contract**\n- Round-trip: every route in the route registry maps to a path and method in the document, and no path in the document lacks a route.\n- Search and tagging operations have example requests and responses, including an empty search result (FR-010).\n- The residency note is present and states EU storage and serving (FR-011).\n- Documented 4xx/5xx responses match observed responses for each endpoint (FR-008). Run after `Q-BE-003` is answered for 401/403.\n- `scope-guard` owns the test that no excluded endpoint exists. This module's contract test only checks that none is *documented*, to avoid re-specifying `scope-guard`.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.test-plan -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.contract-docs.gaps | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Gaps, contradictions, assumptions and questions\n\n### Questions (both `degradable`, unattended: defaults taken)\n\n- **Q-BE-154** (degradable): Must the published document endpoint require authentication? Default taken: no (`ASM-BE-166`).\n- **Q-BE-155** (degradable): For an unsupported Accept header, return 406 or fall back to a default format? Default taken: 406 (`ASM-BE-170`).\n\n### Contradictions\n\n- **CON-BE-155** (non-critical): US-004-03 says \"the send/verify login endpoints document a 429 HTTP status response for more than 3 requests within 10 minutes\". BR-028 and BR-032 limit only requests for a code. The verify refusals in BR-033 are 423, 401, 410 and 409, and none is a rate limit. Documenting 429 on verify would invent behaviour; omitting it breaks the story. Reading adopted: 429 on request-code only, the most specific rules (BR-028, BR-032, BR-033). This also appears as part of `GAP-BE-008`.\n- Existing and relevant: `CON-BE-002`. The status of out-of-scope refusal (404 versus a structured body) affects what the Scope section and error documentation say. Referenced, not re-specified.\n\n### Gaps\n\n| ID | What is missing | Degrades | Fallback | Closing artefact |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `GAP-BE-167` | Field names of the coverage result, and whether it is exposed at runtime or only in the build report. US-004-04 says \"expose\", but no operation record exists for it. | FR-009 | Build-time check only (`ASM-BE-167`) | A decision on exposure and the result shape |\n| `GAP-BE-168` | The third criterion of US-004-03 is garbled (\"a documented field type is changed in code, when the 404 response is checked\"). Its intended check is unclear. | FR-008 | 404 documentation must match runtime behaviour | A clean US-004-03 criterion |\n| `GAP-BE-169` | No input names the CI system, the OpenAPI generation tool or the validator. `platform.md` names Node.js 20, Express, PostgreSQL 16 and AWS only. | Jobs, state machine | Tooling left unnamed | Platform input naming them |\n| `GAP-BE-170` | What the endpoint returns when no document has yet been published. | State machine, FR-001 | Not specified | A decision on this response |\n| `GAP-BE-171` | Constraint values for the schema (title length, tag format) are not given. US-004-02 says they must be documented. | FR-005 | None stated; not invented here (also `GAP-BE-010`) | Constraint decisions from the owning modules |\n| `GAP-BE-172` | Media-type names for the formats, the default format when Accept is absent, and the OpenAPI 3.x minor version. US-004-01 says \"JSON/YAML\" and \"3.x\" only. | FR-001, FR-004 | JSON assumed as default (`ASM-BE-165`) | A format decision |\n\nAlso referenced and unchanged: `GAP-BE-009`, `GAP-BE-010`, `GAP-BE-011`, `GAP-BE-001` (the inbound handoff bodies were not supplied) and `Q-BE-003`.\n\n### Assumptions\n\n- **ASM-BE-164.** This module owns no database tables. Its state is build artefacts. To confirm: the platform owner.\n- **ASM-BE-165.** When Accept is absent or accepts any type, the default format is JSON. US-004-01 names \"JSON/YAML\" but no default (`GAP-BE-172`).\n- **ASM-BE-166.** The published document endpoint is unauthenticated (`Q-BE-154`).\n- **ASM-BE-167.** The document is generated, validated and its coverage computed during the documentation build. The endpoint serves the artefact of the last passing build and does not generate per request.\n- **ASM-BE-168.** The four-state build lifecycle in `backend.contract-docs.state-machine` is derived from the build failures US-004-02, US-004-04 and US-005-01 name.\n- **ASM-BE-169.** Archived and active notes live in the same EU-region store, because archive is a flag and not a move (BR-007). The residency note therefore states no difference between them.\n- **ASM-BE-170.** An unsupported Accept header returns 406. The operation record lists 406 and US-004-01 allows either reading (`Q-BE-155`).\n- **ASM-BE-171.** Review and approval of changes to the out-of-scope list is a repository process (code review), not behaviour of this service.\n- **ASM-BE-172.** Examples in the document use masked placeholders for email addresses and codes. No input supplies example values, so none are reproduced here.\n- **ASM-BE-173.** The path `/openapi` is taken from the operation record, which marks it an assumption to be confirmed. No input names the path.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.contract-docs.gaps -->", "parent_ids": ["US-004-01", "US-004-02", "US-004-03", "US-004-04", "US-004-05", "US-004-06", "US-005-01"]}, {"id": "backend/modules/scope-guard-SPEC.md", "type": "module_spec", "path": "backend/modules/scope-guard-SPEC.md", "content": "<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.frontmatter | status: DERIVED -->\n## Frontmatter\n\n```yaml\nid: scope-guard\nsurface: backend\nspec_mode: NOT-APPLICABLE\ndoc_status: DRAFT\nblocked_by: []\nscope: []\n```\n\n`scope` is empty because DevAgent minted no operation records for this module. It is request middleware over the whole HTTP surface (project `backend.module-map`). It owns refusal behaviour, not endpoints.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.frontmatter -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.purpose | status: DERIVED -->\n## Purpose\n\n**Responsibility.** `scope-guard` keeps notes_api inside its documented scope. It enforces three things:\n\n- **Absence.** No attachment or file-upload endpoint (BR-014), no real-time channel (BR-015), and no mobile-specific endpoint (BR-016) exists.\n- **Refusal.** A call into an excluded category gets a clear, documented, structured refusal, not a silent failure or a bare 404 (BR-016; US-005-04; NFR-011).\n- **Continuous check.** A contract test suite proves that absence on every CI build (NFR-010; US-005-05).\n\n**Owned stories:** US-005-02, US-005-03, US-005-04, US-005-05.\n\n**Not responsible for:**\n\n- Publishing the Scope section of the API documentation, and the docs-build lint that fails when it is missing (US-005-01). `contract-docs` owns these.\n- Documenting the refusal responses in the OpenAPI document. `contract-docs` owns this (US-004-03).\n- Logging and classifying stakeholder requests (BR-017, US-005-06). This is a product-manager process (`GAP-BE-009`).\n- Concurrent-edit semantics of PATCH. The `notes` module defines them. This module only requires that no live broadcast or merge logic exists (US-005-03).\n- Authentication and authorisation (`backend.auth-model`, `BLOCKED` on `Q-BE-003`).\n- Behaviour for paths outside the exclusion categories.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.purpose -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.data-model | status: DERIVED -->\n## Data model\n\nThis module has **no persistent tables, columns, constraints or indexes**.\n\n- Its decision is a pure function of the request: method, path, headers and content type.\n- No input requires it to store anything. Logging and classifying stakeholder requests is a product-manager process (BR-017; `GAP-BE-009`).\n- Refusing a file payload persists no file content (US-005-02).\n\nThe exclusion categories are configuration, not data: attachments, real-time (WebSocket, SSE, polling-push) and mobile-specific (BR-014 to BR-016). Their match patterns are open (`GAP-BE-217`).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.data-model -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.state-machine | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## State machine\n\nThe module is stateless across requests. It has no entity with a status column. Each request has this per-request lifecycle:\n\n| State | Meaning | Terminal |\n|---|---|---|\n| `RECEIVED` | Request arrived at the HTTP surface | no |\n| `PASSED` | Request matches no excluded category and is handed to the owning module | yes, for this module |\n| `REFUSED` | Request matches an excluded category and a structured refusal is returned | yes |\n\n| Transition | Trigger | Guard | Result |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `RECEIVED` \u2192 `REFUSED` | Path matches an attachment pattern, for example `POST /notes/{id}/attachments` | none | 404 with code `OUT_OF_SCOPE` (US-005-02; `ASM-BE-008`) |\n| `RECEIVED` \u2192 `REFUSED` | Multipart or file payload sent to a note create/update endpoint | content type is multipart or carries a file | 415 with a structured error; no file content persisted (US-005-02; US-005-04; `CON-BE-205`) |\n| `RECEIVED` \u2192 `REFUSED` | WebSocket upgrade or SSE connection attempt on any endpoint | none | Connection rejected with no protocol upgrade; status unstated (US-005-03; `GAP-BE-011`) |\n| `RECEIVED` \u2192 `REFUSED` | Call to a mobile-specific path | pattern defined (`GAP-BE-217`) | Documented out-of-scope refusal (BR-016) |\n| `RECEIVED` \u2192 `PASSED` | No category matches | none | Forwarded unchanged |\n\n**Illegal transition.** None exists. Every request ends in exactly one terminal state. `REFUSED` never reverts to `PASSED`.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.state-machine -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.requirements | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Requirements\n\n**BE-SCOPE-GUARD-FR-001**\n- Given the deployed API, when a request is sent to any attachment path (for example `POST /notes/{id}/attachments`), then the API returns 404 and the body carries the structured code `OUT_OF_SCOPE` with a message referencing the scope exclusion, not a bare 404.\n- Traces to. US-005-02 (AC1); US-005-04 (AC1); BR-014; BR-016; `CON-BE-002`; `ASM-BE-008`.\n- Verification. `scope_guard_attachment_path_returns_404_out_of_scope` (planned).\n- API. none.\n\n**BE-SCOPE-GUARD-FR-002**\n- Given a note create or update endpoint, when a multipart/form-data request with a file payload is submitted, then the API returns 415, persists no file content, and the body carries a structured error code and a human-readable reason.\n- Traces to. US-005-02 (AC2); US-005-04 (AC2); `CON-BE-205`.\n- Verification. `scope_guard_multipart_to_notes_returns_415_persists_nothing` (planned).\n- API. none.\n\n**BE-SCOPE-GUARD-FR-003**\n- Given the running API server, when a client attempts a WebSocket upgrade or an SSE connection to any notes_api endpoint, then the connection is rejected, no protocol upgrade occurs, and a standard error status is returned.\n- Traces to. US-005-03 (AC1); BR-015.\n- Verification. `scope_guard_websocket_upgrade_rejected_no_upgrade`; `scope_guard_sse_connection_rejected` (planned).\n- API. none.\n- Open: the status and the detection signature for SSE are unstated (`GAP-BE-011`, `GAP-BE-218`).\n\n**BE-SCOPE-GUARD-FR-004**\n- Given two clients submit PATCH requests to the same note concurrently, when both are processed, then the API applies a standard last-write or conflict response, with no live broadcast and no merge logic.\n- Traces to. US-005-03 (AC2); BR-015.\n- Verification. `scope_guard_concurrent_patch_no_broadcast_no_merge` (planned).\n- API. none. The PATCH operation and its conflict behaviour belong to the `notes` module.\n\n**BE-SCOPE-GUARD-FR-005**\n- Given an integrator calls an out-of-scope endpoint (mobile-specific, attachment or real-time), when the request is made, then the response is a documented 4xx whose body contains a structured error code and a message referencing the scope exclusion.\n- Traces to. US-005-04 (AC1, AC2); BR-016; NFR-011.\n- Verification. `scope_guard_all_excluded_categories_return_documented_4xx` (planned).\n- API. none.\n\n**BE-SCOPE-GUARD-FR-006**\n- Given the CI pipeline runs, when the scope-guardrail test suite executes against the OpenAPI document, then it passes only when zero endpoints match an excluded category (mobile, attachments, real-time).\n- Traces to. US-005-05 (AC1); BR-014; BR-015; NFR-010.\n- Verification. `scope_guard_contract_zero_excluded_endpoints_in_openapi` (planned).\n- API. none.\n\n**BE-SCOPE-GUARD-FR-007**\n- Given a developer adds an endpoint matching an excluded pattern (for example `/attachments` or `/ws`), when CI runs on the pull request, then the guardrail test fails and blocks the merge.\n- Traces to. US-005-05 (AC2); NFR-010.\n- Verification. `scope_guard_contract_fails_on_added_excluded_endpoint` (planned).\n- API. none.\n\n**BE-SCOPE-GUARD-FR-008**\n- Given any refusal produced by this module, when the response body is built, then it contains no login code, no unmasked email address and no echo of the request payload.\n- Traces to. NFR-018; `ASM-BE-215`.\n- Verification. `scope_guard_refusal_body_has_no_payload_echo` (planned).\n- API. none.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.requirements -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.api-contract | status: VERIFIED -->\n## Api contract\n\n_Rendered by DevAgent from the operation records._\n\nNo HTTP operation is defined.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.api-contract -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.invariants | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Invariants\n\n- **INV-BE-201.** After every build, the route registry and the published OpenAPI document contain no endpoint in an excluded category (attachments, real-time, mobile-specific). Source: BR-014 to BR-016; NFR-010.\n- **INV-BE-202.** After any refused request, no file content has been persisted. Source: US-005-02.\n- **INV-BE-203.** No request on any endpoint ever results in a protocol upgrade to WebSocket or SSE. Source: US-005-03; BR-015.\n- **INV-BE-204.** Every refusal returned by this module carries a structured error code and a message that references the scope exclusion. Source: US-005-04; BR-016; NFR-011.\n- **INV-BE-205.** No refusal body contains a login code, an unmasked email address or an echo of the request payload. Source: NFR-018; `ASM-BE-215`.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.invariants -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.authorization | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Authorization\n\nThis module owns no operation, so there is no role \u00d7 operation matrix.\n\n- **Rule.** Refusals apply to every caller. No role may reach an excluded endpoint, because none exists (BR-014 to BR-016, all with \"Exception: None\").\n- **Ordering.** Refusal is evaluated independently of authentication, so an unauthenticated call to an excluded path gets the same refusal as an authenticated one (`ASM-BE-214`). Whether other endpoints require authentication depends on `Q-BE-003`.\n- **Ownership predicate.** None. This module touches no user-scoped resource.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.authorization -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.jobs | status: DERIVED -->\n## Jobs\n\n- **Scheduled work.** None at runtime. No input requires a sweep or retry in this module.\n- **CI check.** The contract test suite runs on every CI build (NFR-010; BE-SCOPE-GUARD-FR-006, FR-007). It is a build step, not a scheduled job.\n- **Idempotency.** Refusals have no side effects, so repeating a refused request returns the same refusal. No idempotency key is needed.\n- **Retries.** None.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.jobs -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.test-plan | status: UNKNOWN -->\n## Test plan\n\nAll tests below are planned. None exists yet.\n\n**Unit**\n- `scope_guard_matcher_classifies_attachment_paths`: `/notes/{id}/attachments` and `/attachments` match the attachment category.\n- `scope_guard_matcher_classifies_realtime_paths`: `/ws` matches the real-time category.\n- `scope_guard_matcher_passes_in_scope_paths`: `/notes`, `/notes/{id}`, `/notes/search`, `/auth/request-code` and `/auth/verify-code` are not matched.\n- `scope_guard_refusal_body_shape`: the body has a code and a message, and no payload echo (INV-BE-204, INV-BE-205).\n\n**Integration**\n- `scope_guard_attachment_path_returns_404_out_of_scope` (FR-001)\n- `scope_guard_multipart_to_notes_returns_415_persists_nothing` (FR-002)\n- `scope_guard_websocket_upgrade_rejected_no_upgrade` and `scope_guard_sse_connection_rejected` (FR-003)\n- `scope_guard_concurrent_patch_no_broadcast_no_merge` (FR-004)\n- `scope_guard_all_excluded_categories_return_documented_4xx` (FR-005)\n\n**Contract**\n- `scope_guard_contract_zero_excluded_endpoints_in_openapi` (FR-006)\n- `scope_guard_contract_fails_on_added_excluded_endpoint` (FR-007)\n- `scope_guard_contract_zero_excluded_routes_in_registry`: runs the same check against the route registry (`ASM-BE-216`).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.test-plan -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.scope-guard.gaps | status: VERIFIED -->\n## Gaps\n\n**Assumptions**\n- `ASM-BE-214`: Refusal of excluded paths is evaluated before and independent of authentication. Confirmed by: the outcome of `Q-BE-003`.\n- `ASM-BE-215`: A refusal body uses the proposed envelope (`ASM-BE-007`: `code` and `message`) and never echoes the request payload. Confirmed by: an error-model decision (`GAP-BE-011`).\n- `ASM-BE-216`: The contract suite checks both the OpenAPI document (US-005-05) and the route registry (BR-014, BR-015). Confirmed by: a statement of the suite's input.\n\n**Contradictions**\n- `CON-BE-205` (non-critical): The project error-model proposes `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE` for 415 (`ASM-BE-007`). US-005-04 AC2 gives `OUT_OF_SCOPE` as the example code for a multipart upload, and US-005-02 AC2 fixes the status at 415. Reading adopted: status 415 with code `OUT_OF_SCOPE`, because it satisfies both stories' text. If `OUT_OF_SCOPE` is only an example, `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE` remains valid. A decision on the 415 code name would close it. Related: `CON-BE-002`.\n\n**Gaps**\n- `GAP-BE-217` (non-critical): No input defines the match patterns for the excluded categories. Only examples exist: `/notes/{id}/attachments`, `/attachments` and `/ws`. There is no definition of \"mobile-specific\" or of a \"polling-push\" channel (BR-015). This degrades FR-003, FR-005, FR-006 and FR-007, which rely on a concrete exclusion list. Closing artefact: the documented exclusion list, which the Scope section (US-005-01) may supply.\n- `GAP-BE-218` (non-critical): No input says how an SSE attempt is recognised, since it is an ordinary GET. This degrades FR-003. Closing artefact: a detection rule, such as the request signature that counts as an SSE attempt.\n\n**Referenced, not re-minted**\n- `GAP-BE-011`: the rejection status for WebSocket and SSE attempts, and the final error-code names.\n- `GAP-BE-009`: no module owns BR-017 and US-005-06.\n- `CON-BE-002`: the out-of-scope status (404 versus \"not a bare 404\").\n- `Q-BE-003`: the session decision.\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.scope-guard.gaps -->", "parent_ids": ["US-005-02", "US-005-03", "US-005-04", "US-005-05"]}, {"id": "backend/modules/residency-SPEC.md", "type": "module_spec", "path": "backend/modules/residency-SPEC.md", "content": "<!-- SECTION: backend.residency.frontmatter | status: VERIFIED -->\n## Frontmatter\n\n```yaml\nid: residency\nsurface: backend\nspec_mode: NOT-APPLICABLE\ndoc_status: DRAFT\nblocked_by: []\nscope: [API-RESIDENCY-026899, API-RESIDENCY-174124, API-RESIDENCY-685134, API-RESIDENCY-913995, API-RESIDENCY-648672, API-RESIDENCY-922826, API-RESIDENCY-202775]\n```\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.residency.frontmatter -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.residency.purpose | status: DERIVED -->\n## Purpose\n\n**Responsibility.** The `residency` module proves and enforces that every `notes_api` infrastructure component (database, compute, backups) sits in an approved EU region. Sources: BR-001 to BR-004; NFR-001 to NFR-004; `backend.module-map`. It covers six concerns, each with its owning operations:\n\n| Concern | Stories | Operation |\n|---|---|---|\n| Refuse provisioning in a non-EU region | US-001-01 | `API-RESIDENCY-026899` |\n| Validate a backup destination before a backup runs | US-001-02 | `API-RESIDENCY-174124` |\n| Evaluate the CI residency gate per deploy | US-001-03 | `API-RESIDENCY-685134` |\n| Report region and compliance status per component | US-001-04 | `API-RESIDENCY-913995` |\n| Run the launch gate check | US-001-05 | `API-RESIDENCY-648672` |\n| Scan for drift after launch and alert DevOps; read scan results | US-001-06 | `API-RESIDENCY-922826`, `API-RESIDENCY-202775` |\n\n**Not responsible for:**\n\n- Application behaviour of notes, search or login. Those belong to `notes`, `search` and `auth`.\n- The OpenAPI document and its residency note (US-004-06, BR-013). That belongs to `contract-docs`.\n- Residency of login emails (NFR-017). That is an `auth` concern.\n- Human sign-off of the launch gate (BR-003 Enforcement: \"Launch gate sign-off by Engineering Lead\"). This module computes a verdict only (`CON-BE-257`).\n- Creating cloud resources. Region constraints in infrastructure-as-code are the BR-001 enforcement. Whether the provisioning operation also creates anything is open (`GAP-BE-272`).\n- Defining the approved region list. It is data, and no input supplies it (`GAP-BE-267`).\n- The identity mechanism for callers. That depends on `backend.auth-model`, which is BLOCKED on `Q-BE-003` (`GAP-BE-278`).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.residency.purpose -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.residency.data-model | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Data model\n\nPersistence is in the notes_api PostgreSQL 16 database (`platform.md`; `ASM-BE-264`). Conventions under `ASM-BE-264`: identifiers are `uuid` generated by the database, timestamps are `timestamptz` in UTC, and every foreign key is `ON DELETE RESTRICT` because no input defines deletion of residency records (`GAP-BE-273`). No column holds PII, money or a credential.\n\n### `residency_approved_regions`\n\n| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraint |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `region_code` | text | NOT NULL | none | PRIMARY KEY |\n| `added_at` | timestamptz | NOT NULL | `now()` | none |\n\nThe table is empty until the region list is supplied (`GAP-BE-267`). Every comparison in this module reads this table, so no region is hard-coded.\n\n### `residency_resources`\n\n| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraint |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `id` | uuid | NOT NULL | database-generated | PRIMARY KEY |\n| `external_ref` | text | NOT NULL | none | UNIQUE (`ASM-BE-271`) |\n| `component_name` | text | NOT NULL | none | none |\n| `resource_type` | text | NOT NULL | none | none |\n| `region` | text | NULL | none | NULL means region metadata is missing or unreadable (AC-001-03-03) |\n| `first_seen_at` | timestamptz | NOT NULL | `now()` | none |\n| `last_seen_at` | timestamptz | NOT NULL | `now()` | none |\n\nThe unique index on `external_ref` serves matching CI-gate targets and scan discoveries to existing rows.\n\n### `residency_scans`\n\n| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraint |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `id` | uuid | NOT NULL | database-generated | PRIMARY KEY |\n| `kind` | text | NOT NULL | none | CHECK in (`drift`, `launch-gate`, `ci-gate`) |\n| `state` | text | NOT NULL | `running` | CHECK in (`running`, `completed`, `failed`) |\n| `build_ref` | text | NULL | none | used by `ci-gate` only (`GAP-BE-277`) |\n| `verdict` | text | NULL | none | CHECK in (`pass`, `fail`); NOT NULL when `state` is `completed` |\n| `total_count` | integer | NULL | none | NOT NULL when `completed` |\n| `pass_count` | integer | NULL | none | NOT NULL when `completed` |\n| `fail_count` | integer | NULL | none | NOT NULL when `completed` |\n| `unverified_count` | integer | NULL | none | NOT NULL when `completed` |\n| `started_at` | timestamptz | NOT NULL | `now()` | none |\n| `completed_at` | timestamptz | NULL | none | NOT NULL when `state` is `completed` or `failed` |\n\nCHECK: when `completed`, `pass_count + fail_count + unverified_count = total_count` (`INV-BE-256`). Percent compliant is derived, never stored (`ASM-BE-266`).\n\nIndex: `(kind, completed_at DESC) WHERE state = 'completed'`. It serves \"latest completed scan of a kind\" for the compliance report and the launch gate.\n\n### `residency_scan_results`\n\n| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraint |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `scan_id` | uuid | NOT NULL | none | FK \u2192 `residency_scans.id` |\n| `resource_id` | uuid | NOT NULL | none | FK \u2192 `residency_resources.id` |\n| `observed_region` | text | NULL | none | NULL when missing or unreadable |\n| `status` | text | NOT NULL | none | CHECK in (`pass`, `fail`, `unverified`) (`ASM-BE-265`) |\n\nPRIMARY KEY `(scan_id, resource_id)`. Index: `(scan_id, status)`, which serves listing the non-passing resources of a scan.\n\n### `residency_alerts`\n\n| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraint |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `id` | uuid | NOT NULL | database-generated | PRIMARY KEY |\n| `scan_id` | uuid | NOT NULL | none | FK \u2192 `residency_scans.id` |\n| `resource_id` | uuid | NOT NULL | none | FK \u2192 `residency_resources.id` |\n| `detected_at` | timestamptz | NOT NULL | `now()` | none |\n| `sent_at` | timestamptz | NULL | none | NOT NULL when `delivery_state` is `sent` |\n| `delivery_state` | text | NOT NULL | `pending` | CHECK in (`pending`, `sent`, `failed`) |\n\nUNIQUE `(scan_id, resource_id)` (`ASM-BE-269`). Index: `(delivery_state, detected_at) WHERE delivery_state <> 'sent'`, which serves the dispatch sweep.\n\n### `residency_provisioning_requests`\n\n| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraint |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `id` | uuid | NOT NULL | database-generated | PRIMARY KEY |\n| `resource_type` | text | NOT NULL | none | none |\n| `requested_region` | text | NULL | none | NULL when the request supplied none |\n| `outcome` | text | NOT NULL | none | CHECK in (`accepted`, `rejected`) |\n| `rejection_reason` | text | NULL | none | NOT NULL when `rejected` |\n| `resource_id` | uuid | NULL | none | FK \u2192 `residency_resources.id`; always NULL when `rejected` (`INV-BE-254`) |\n| `created_at` | timestamptz | NOT NULL | `now()` | none |\n\nNo index beyond the primary key. No input names a query on this table.\n\n### `residency_backup_validations`\n\n| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraint |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `id` | uuid | NOT NULL | database-generated | PRIMARY KEY |\n| `destination_ref` | text | NOT NULL | none | a reference only; never a credential (`INV-BE-257`) |\n| `destination_region` | text | NULL | none | NULL when it cannot be determined |\n| `outcome` | text | NOT NULL | none | CHECK in (`pass`, `fail`) |\n| `elapsed_ms` | integer | NOT NULL | none | CHECK \u2265 0 |\n| `created_at` | timestamptz | NOT NULL | `now()` | none |\n\nNo index beyond the primary key.\n\n**Open.** The project `backend.migration-plan` has no migration for these tables (`GAP-BE-275`). Encryption at rest is unstated (`GAP-BE-012`).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.residency.data-model -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.residency.state-machine | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## State machine\n\nTwo stored entities have a status column. Nothing in the listed operations lets a caller set a state directly. All transitions are made by this module (`ASM-BE-272`).\n\n### Scan (`residency_scans.state`)\n\nStates: `running`, `completed`, `failed`. Terminal: `completed`, `failed`.\n\n| From | To | Trigger | Guard |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| (new) | `running` | `API-RESIDENCY-922826`, `API-RESIDENCY-685134` or `API-RESIDENCY-648672` accepted | none |\n| `running` | `completed` | every in-scope resource has a result row | counts sum to `total_count`; `verdict` set; `completed_at` set |\n| `running` | `failed` | inventory unreadable, or internal error | `completed_at` set; `verdict` stays NULL |\n\nA `failed` scan never counts as a pass. A gate caller treats it as blocked, by the same reasoning as AC-001-03-03 (`ASM-BE-266`).\n\nIllegal transitions, such as anything out of a terminal state, are rejected inside the module and logged. No API status is defined for them because no operation can request one (`ASM-BE-272`).\n\n### Alert (`residency_alerts.delivery_state`)\n\nStates: `pending`, `sent`, `failed`. Terminal: `sent`, and `failed` for now.\n\n| From | To | Trigger | Guard |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| (new) | `pending` | scan completes with a `fail` result for a resource | one alert per `(scan, resource)` |\n| `pending` | `sent` | delivery to DevOps succeeds | `sent_at` set |\n| `pending` | `failed` | delivery fails | none |\n\n`failed` could return to `pending` only under a retry policy, and none is stated (`GAP-BE-270`).\n\n### Launch gate (derived, not stored)\n\nThe gate is \"passed\" when the latest completed `launch-gate` scan has verdict `pass`. Otherwise it is \"failed\" and release is blocked until a later check passes (AC-001-05-01, AC-001-05-02). A past verdict is never changed; a new check creates a new scan (`ASM-BE-273`).\n<!-- /SECTION: backend.residency.state-machine -->\n<!-- SECTION: backend.residency.requirements | status: DRAFT-UNVERIFIED -->\n## Requirements\n\nPer-resource status values (`pass`, `fail`, `unverified`) are defined in `ASM-BE-265`. The approved list is `residency_approved_regions` (`GAP-BE-267`).\n\n**BE-RESIDENCY-FR-001 \u2014 Accept provisioning in an approved EU region**\n- Given a provisioning request whose target region is in the approved EU list, when it is submitted, then the request is accepted and recorded with outcome `accepted`.\n- **Traces to.** US-001-01; AC-001-01-01; BR-001\n- **Verification.** `residency.provisioning.accepts_eu_region` (planned)\n- **API.** `API-RESIDENCY-026899`\n\n**BE-RESIDENCY-FR-002 \u2014 Refuse provisioning in a non-EU region**\n- Given a provisioning request whose target region is not in the approved EU list, when it is submitted, then it is rejected with a residency violation error, no resource is created and none is recorded.\n- **Traces to.** US-001-01; AC-001-01-02; BR-001\n- **Verification.** `residency.provisioning.rejects_non_eu_region` (planned)\n- **API.** `API-RESIDENCY-026899`\n\n**BE-RESIDENCY-FR-003 \u2014 Refuse provisioning when the region is absent or unrecognised**\n- Given a provisioning request with no region or an unrecognised region value, when it is submitted, then it is rejected as in FR-002. This is derived from BR-001 \"exclusively in approved EU regions\", with fail-closed handling as in AC-001-03-03.\n- **Traces to.** US-001-01; BR-001\n- **Verification.** `residency.provisioning.rejects_missing_region` (planned)\n- **API.** `API-RESIDENCY-026899`\n\n**BE-RESIDENCY-FR-004 \u2014 Accept an EU backup destination**\n- Given a backup destination in an approved EU region, when it is validated, then validation passes and the backup may proceed, so the artifact lands in EU storage.\n- **Traces to.** US-001-02; AC-001-02-01; BR-001\n- **Verification.** `residency.backup.accepts_eu_destination` (planned)\n- **API.** `API-RESIDENCY-174124`\n\n**BE-RESIDENCY-FR-005 \u2014 Fail a non-EU backup destination fast**\n- Given a backup destination outside the EU, when validation runs before the backup, then the job fails within 30 seconds with a residency violation error and writes no data.\n- **Traces to.** US-001-02; AC-001-02-02\n- **Verification.** `residency.backup.rejects_non_eu_within_30s` (planned)\n- **API.** `API-RESIDENCY-174124`\n\n**BE-RESIDENCY-FR-006 \u2014 CI gate passes when all targets are EU**\n- Given a deployment whose target resources are all in EU regions, when the gate is evaluated, then the verdict is `pass` and the deployment proceeds.\n- **Traces to.** US-001-03;
